Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n    12→\n    13→3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING WHEN APPLICABLE**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference paper and code, except where the decoder input and output specifications or training a neural decoder require otherwise. You will be assessed on the statistics and decoder accuracy of your converted data, and on whether an expert would consider each of your decisions well justified.\n    14→\n    15→---\n    16→\n    17→## Project Context\n    18→\n    19→- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository when applicable. This requires examining the provided paper, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **paper** \"Identifying representational structure in CA1 to benchmark theoretical models of cognitive mapping\" is in the file `/app/paper.pdf`.\n    27→- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n    28→- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `/app/code`.\n    29→- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `/app/data`.\n    30→\n    31→## Decoder Task\n    32→Decode the location of the mice from the neural activities data recorded in the CA1 region.\n    33→The experiment consists of long recording sessions, which will be split into 1-minute trials within each session.\n    34→\n    35→### Decoder Inputs:\n    36→- Environment geometry, representing which parts of the arena are blocked. Static per-trial.\n    37→\n    38→### Decoder Outputs\n    39→- Mouse position discretized into 3 x 3 = 9 spatial bins. Time-varying.\n    40→\n    41→## Target Data Format\n    42→\n    43→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n    44→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    45→\n    46→```python\n    47→data = {\n    48→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    49→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    50→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    51→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    52→            ...\n    53→        ],\n    54→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    55→            ...\n    56→        ],\n    57→        ...\n    58→    ],\n    59→\n    60→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    61→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    62→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        ...\n    66→    ],\n    67→\n    68→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    71→            ...\n    72→        ],\n    73→        ...\n    74→    ],\n    75→\n    76→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    77→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    78→\n    79→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    80→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    81→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    82→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    83→\n    84→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    85→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    86→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    87→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    88→        ...\n    89→    ],\n    90→\n    91→    'metadata': {\n    92→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n    93→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n    94→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n    95→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n    96→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n    97→        ...\n    98→    }\n    99→}\n   100→```\n   101→\n   102→### Key Format Requirements\n   103→\n   104→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   105→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   106→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   107→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   108→\n   109→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   110→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   111→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   112→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   113→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   114→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   115→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   116→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   117→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   118→\n   119→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   120→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   121→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   122→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   123→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   124→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   125→\n   126→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   127→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   128→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   129→\n   130→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   131→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   132→\n   133→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   134→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   135→\n   136→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   137→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   138→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   139→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   140→\n   141→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   142→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   143→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   144→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   145→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   146→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   147→\n   148→## Python environment\n   149→\n   150→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   151→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   152→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   153→\n   154→## Decoder Reference\n   155→\n   156→- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   157→- Optional arguments:\n   158→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   159→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   160→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   161→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   162→\n   163→### Purpose\n   164→\n   165→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   166→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   167→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   168→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   169→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   170→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   171→\n   172→### Consistency requirements\n   173→\n   174→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   175→- Loading of data\n   176→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   177→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   178→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   179→\n   180→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   181→\n   182→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   183→\n   184→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder.\n   185→\n   186→Carefully verify your work after every step. Experimental and behavioral variables\n   291→  - Data processing\n   292→  - Data curation\n   293→- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n   294→\n   295→**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n   296→\n   297→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   298→\n   299→---\n   300→\n   301→### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   302→\n   303→**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n   304→\n   305→**Actions**:\n   306→- Map source data elements to target format components:\n   307→  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n   308→  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n   309→  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   310→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   311→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   312→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   313→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `/app/code` directory. \n   314→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   315→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   316→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   317→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   318→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   319→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   320→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   321→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   322→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   323→\n   324→**Done when**:\n   325→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   326→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   327→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   328→\n   329→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   330→\n   331→---\n   332→\n   333→### Step 6: Script Development\n   334→\n   335→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `/app/convert_data.py`.\n   336→\n   337→**Actions**:\n   338→- Write all conversion code in the file `/app/convert_data.py`\n   339→- Script should run as **`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   340→- Include options:\n   341→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   342→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   343→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   344→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   345→- Write efficient code:\n   346→  - Vectorize loops\n   347→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   348→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   349→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   350→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   351→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   352→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   353→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   354→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   355→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   356→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   357→\n   358→**Done when**: `/app/convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   359→\n   360→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `/app/convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   361→\n   362→---\n   363→\n   364→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   365→\n   366→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   367→\n   368→**Actions**:\n   369→1. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   370→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   371→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   372→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   373→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   374→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   375→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   376→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   377→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   378→3. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   379→4. Analyze the output file `/app/verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   380→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   381→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   382→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   383→\n   384→**Done when**: \n   385→- `/app/sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   386→- `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   387→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   388→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   389→\n   390→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `/app/sample_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   391→\n   392→---\n   393→\n   394→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   395→\n   396→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   397→\n   398→**Actions**:\n   399→1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   400→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   401→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   402→\n   403→**Investigate any issues**:\n   404→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   405→\n   406→**Done when**: \n   407→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   408→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   409→- Accuracies are above chance\n   410→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   411→\n   412→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   413→\n   414→---\n   415→\n   416→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   417→\n   418→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   419→\n   420→**Actions**:\n   421→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   422→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   423→3. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_full_out.txt`\n   424→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   425→5. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_full_out.txt`. \n   426→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   427→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   428→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `/app/verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   429→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   430→\n   431→**Done when**:\n   432→- `/app/converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   433→- `/app/verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   434→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   435→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   436→\n   437→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `/app/converted_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   438→\n   439→---\n   440→\n   441→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   442→\n   443→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   444→\n   445→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   446→Read through `/app/verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   447→\n   448→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   449→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   450→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   451→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   452→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   453→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   454→- Document your sanity checks to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   455→\n   456→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   457→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   458→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   459→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   460→- Fix any mismatches. \n   461→- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   462→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   463→\n   464→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   465→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \n   466→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   467→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   468→- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   469→\n   470→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   471→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   472→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   473→\n   474→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   475→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   476→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   477→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   478→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   479→\n   480→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   481→\n   482→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   483→\n   484→---\n   485→\n   486→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   487→\n   488→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   489→\n   490→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   491→\n   492→**Actions**:\n   493→1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   494→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   495→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   496→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \n   497→\n   498→**Done when**: \n   499→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   500→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   501→\n   502→---\n   503→\n   504→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   505→\n   506→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   507→\n   508→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   509→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   510→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   511→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   512→\n   513→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\n   514→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\n   515→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\n   516→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   517→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   518→\n   519→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   520→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   521→\n   522→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   523→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   524→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   525→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   526→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   527→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \n   528→\n   529→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   530→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   531→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   532→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   533→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   534→\n   535→**Report all checks and results** to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   536→\n   537→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   538→\n   539→---\n   540→\n   541→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   542→\n   543→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   544→\n   545→**Actions**:\n   546→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   547→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   548→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   549→  - Validation results and tables\n   550→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   551→- Create a user-facing `/app/README.md` summarizing:\n   552→  - Dataset description\n   553→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   554→  - Output format specification\n   555→  - Key statistics\n   556→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `/app/cache/` folder\n   557→- Create `/app/cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   558→\n   559→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   560→\n   561→---\n   562→\n   563→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   564→\n   565→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. data\n   817→- **Trial alignment**: Align trials to meaningful events (stimulus onset, movement, etc.)\n   818→- **Time windows**: Choose appropriate pre/post-event windows\n   819→- **Neuron curation**: Choose filtering criteria for the quality of each neuron's recording based on reference texts\n   820→- **Trial curation**: Choose quality filtering criteria for trials based on reference texts or bad or missing data\n   821→\n   822→### Computational Efficiency\n   823→- Be mindful of memory usage with large datasets\n   824→- Use appropriate data types (float32 vs. float64)\n   825→- Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING WHEN APPLICABLE**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference paper and code, except where the decoder input and output specifications or training a neural decoder require otherwise. You will be assessed on the statistics and decoder accuracy of your converted data, and on whether an expert would consider each of your decisions well justified.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository when applicable. This requires examining the provided paper, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"Identifying representational structure in CA1 to benchmark theoretical models of cognitive mapping\" is in the file `/app/paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `/app/code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `/app/data`.\n\n## Decoder Task\nDecode the location of the mice from the neural activities data recorded in the CA1 region.\nThe experiment consists of long recording sessions, which will be split into 1-minute trials within each session.\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Environment geometry, representing which parts of the arena are blocked. Static per-trial.\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Mouse position discretized into 3 x 3 = 9 spatial bins. Time-varying.\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder.\n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. Neural data is derived from the `trace` variable in the `.mat` file, which contains calcium traces with shape `(timepoints, neurons)`.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrace = f[trace_refs[i][0]][:]  # (timepoints, neurons)\n```\n\niii. The `trace` variable contains the pre-processed calcium imaging traces stored by the original authors.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. The only processing is transposing from `(timepoints, neurons)` to `(neurons, timepoints)` and casting to float32. The traces are already deconvolved spike data, so no additional processing is needed. Neurons that are not present in the current recording session are filtered out (see 2-c).\n\nii.\n```python\ntrace = trace[:, active_mask].astype(np.float32).T  # (n_active, n_timepoints)\n```\n\niii. The original authors already converted from raw calcium fluorescence to deconvolved spikes. No further processing was applied beyond transposing to the `(neurons, time)` convention.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Neurons that are all-NaN (not recorded in that session) are removed. Only neurons with at least one valid value are kept.\n\nii.\n```python\nactive_mask = ~np.all(np.isnan(trace), axis=0)\ntrace = trace[:, active_mask].astype(np.float32).T\n```\n\niii. The `trace` array contains NaN columns for neurons not recorded in a given session (the same animal may have different neurons active across sessions). Filtering by all-NaN removes these unrecorded neurons.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. No event-based alignment. The recording is continuous, and trials are artificial 60-second segments.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. There is no stimulus event to align to.\n\n## 2-e. How is the `neural` data temporally binned/resampled?\n\ni. The neural data is kept at the native 30 Hz frame rate. No resampling is applied.\n\nii.\n```python\nSAMPLING_RATE = 30  # Hz\nTIME_BIN_SIZE = 1000.0 / SAMPLING_RATE  # ~33.33 ms\n```\n\niii. The data is already at a consistent 30 Hz frame rate. No resampling is needed.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Blocked positions* derived from?\n\ni. Blocked positions are derived from the `blocked` variable in the `.mat` file, which contains indices of blocked reward locations for each recording session.\n\nii.\n```python\nblk_refs = f['blocked'][:][0]\n...\nblk_indices = f[blk_refs[i]][:].flatten()\n```\n\niii. The `blocked` variable stores which of the 9 possible reward positions were blocked (unavailable) during each session. A value of `[-1]` indicates no positions were blocked.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Blocked positions*?\n\ni. Blocked indices are converted to a one-hot encoding over 9 possible positions. If no positions are blocked (`[-1]`), the vector is all zeros. The blocked vector is constant across all timepoints and trials within a session.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef encode_blocked(blk_indices, n_positions=N_BLOCKED_POSITIONS):\n    blocked = np.zeros(n_positions, dtype=np.float32)\n    if not (len(blk_indices) == 1 and blk_indices[0] == -1):\n        blocked[blk_indices.astype(int)] = 1\n    return blocked\n...\ninput_trials = [blocked] * len(neural_trials)\n```\n\niii. One-hot encoding allows the decoder to treat each blocked position independently. The encoding is per-session (constant across trials) since blocked positions don't change within a session.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Position* derived from?\n\ni. Position is derived from the `position` variable in the `.mat` file, which contains 2D coordinates `(x, y)` of the animal in the arena.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = f[pos_refs[i][0]][:].T  # (2, n_timepoints)\n```\n\niii. The `position` variable records the animal's location in a 75×75 cm open field arena at each timepoint.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Position*?\n\ni. The 2D position is discretized into a 3×3 grid (9 classes). Each axis is divided into 3 equal bins spanning the 75 cm arena. The grid label is computed as `y_bin * 3 + x_bin`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position, n_grid=N_GRID, arena_size=ARENA_SIZE):\n    edges = np.linspace(0, arena_size, n_grid + 1)[1:-1]\n    x_bin = np.clip(np.digitize(position[0], edges), 0, n_grid - 1)\n    y_bin = np.clip(np.digitize(position[1], edges), 0, n_grid - 1)\n    return (y_bin * n_grid + x_bin).astype(np.int8)\n```\n\niii. A 3×3 grid gives 9 position classes, providing a coarse but balanced spatial discretization. `np.clip` ensures positions at the arena boundaries are assigned to valid bins.\n\n## 4-d. How is `output` *Position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Position and neural data are stored at the same frame rate in the `.mat` file, so they are aligned frame-for-frame. Both are split into trials using the same indices.\n\nii.\n```python\nneural_trials = split_into_trials(trace)\noutput_trials = split_into_trials(output)\n```\n\niii. Both arrays have the same number of timepoints and are sliced identically by `split_into_trials`, ensuring alignment.\n\n## 5. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Neurons with all-NaN values (not recorded in that session) are removed (see 2-c). Remainder frames that don't fill a complete 60-second trial are discarded.\n\nii.\n```python\nactive_mask = ~np.all(np.isnan(trace), axis=0)\ntrace = trace[:, active_mask].astype(np.float32).T\n...\nn_trials = data.shape[1] // trial_length  # remainder is implicitly dropped\n```\n\niii. NaN filtering ensures only actually recorded neurons are included. Discarding remainder frames is a minor data loss.\n\n## 6-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. 5 s of running; trials with less are dropped\n    66→MIN_TRIALS_PER_SESSION = 2 # train_validate_decoder needs >= 2 trials to split a session\n    67→\n    68→# Two sessions used by --sample, chosen from two different animals and two geometries\n    69→# so that the sample exercises subject indexing and a non-square geometry.\n    70→SAMPLE_SESSIONS = [(\"QLAK-CA1-08\", 0), (\"QLAK-CA1-30\", 3)]\n    71→\n    72→BRAIN_REGIONS = [\"CA1\"]    # Verified in Step 4 to equal `flipud(get_env_mat(env)) == 0`\n    85→    for every session, and to coincide exactly with the partitions the animal never\n    86→    visits.\n    87→\n    88→    Returns:\n    89→        geometry: (9,) float32, 1.0 where the partition is blocked.\n    90→    \"\"\"\n    91→    idx = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(blocked_day, dtype=float).ravel())\n    92→    geometry = np.zeros(SPATIAL_BINS * SPATIAL_BINS, dtype=np.float32)\n    93→    if idx.size == 1 and idx[0] < 0:          # -1 => open square, nothing blocked\n    94→        return geometry\n    95→    geometry[idx.astype(int)] = 1.0\n    96→    return geometry\n    97→\n    98→\n    99→def compute_speed(position_xy):\n   100→    \"\"\"Running speed in cm/s, exactly as in `decode_position_within`.\n   101→\n   102→    Args:\n   103→        position_xy: (2, T) position in cm.\n   104→    Returns:\n   105→        (T,) speed; element 0 is 0 and is always excluded downstream, matching the\n   106→        reference where `vel_idx[0]` is left False.\n   107→    \"\"\"\n   108→    step = np.linalg.norm(np.diff(position_xy, axis=1), axis=0) * FPS   # (T-1,) cm/s\n   109→    speed = np.zeros(position_xy.shape[1], dtype=np.float64)\n   110→    speed[1:] = gaussian_filter1d(step, sigma=V_FILT_SIGMA)\n   111→    return speed\n   112→\n   113→\n   114→def bin_time(x, n_trials, axis=-1, reduce=\"mean\"):\n   115→    \"\"\"Average-pool the last axis into (n_trials, BINS_PER_TRIAL) blocks of\n   116→    TEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES frames.  Equivalent to torch AvgPool1d(kernel=stride=bin) applied\n   117→    within each 1-minute trial.\"\"\"\n   118→    x = np.moveaxis(x, axis, -1)\n   119→    x = x[..., : n_trials * TRIAL_FRAMES]\n   120→    shp = x.shape[:-1] + (n_trials, BINS_PER_TRIAL, TEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES)\n   121→    out = x.reshape(shp).mean(axis=-1)\n   122→    return out\n   123→\n   124→\n   125→# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   126→# Per-session conversion\n   127→# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   128→\n   129→\n   130→def convert_session(trace_day, position_day, blocked_day, env_name, bin_down,\n   131→                    session_id, show_processing=False, plot_dir=\".\"):\n   132→    \"\"\"Convert a single animal-day recording into lists of per-trial arrays.\n   133→\n   134→    Args:\n   135→        trace_day: (n_cells_total, T) binarized calcium events, NaN for cells that were\n   136→                   not registered on this day.\n   137→        position_day: (2, T) x-y position in cm.\n   138→        blocked_day: the session's entry of the dataset `blocked` field.\n   139→        env_name: geometry name (for bookkeeping / plots).\n   140→        bin_down: spatial bin width in cm (shared across all sessions of the animal).\n   141→        session_id: string used for plot filenames.\n   142→    Returns:\n   143→        dict with 'neural', 'input', 'output' (lists of per-trial arrays), plus\n   144→        bookkeeping fields, or None if the session does not survive curation.\n   145→    \"\"\"\n   146→    n_cells_total, n_frames = trace_day.shape\n   147→    n_trials = n_frames // TRIAL_FRAMES          #   The second term removes rare tracking artifacts: a handful of frames (and, on\n   170→    #   two sessions, a few hundred) are tracked inside a partition that is walled off,\n   171→    #   which cannot happen physically.  These would be unlearnable label noise.\n   172→    moving = speed_binned > V_THRESH\n   173→    reachable = geometry[partition] == 0\n   174→    keep = moving & reachable\n   175→    keep[0, 0] = False          # frame/bin 0 has no defined speed (reference excludes it)\n   176→\n   177→    # ---- 6. cell activity filter ------------------------------------------------------\n   178→    #   decode_position_within: cells with more than `cell_threshold` events among the\n   179→    #   retained samples. Expressed in Hz (x FPS) as in get_rate_maps.\n   189→    smoothed = gaussian_filter1d(raw[active], sigma=TEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES, axis=-1)\n   190→    neural_binned = bin_time(smoothed, n_trials) * FPS           # (n_act, n_trials, BINS)\n   191→    del smoothed\n   192→\n   193→    # ---- 8. assemble per-trial arrays -------------------------------------------------\n   194→    neural_trials, continue\n   199→        neural_trials.append(np.ascontiguousarray(neural_binned[:, t, sel], dtype=np.float32))\n   200→        input_trials.append(geometry.copy())\n   201→        output_trials.append(partition[t, sel][None, :].astype(np.int64))\n   202→        trial_index.append(t)\n   203→\n   204→    if len(neural_trials) < MIN_TRIALS_PER_SESSION:\n   205→        return \"n_frames_total\": n_frames,\n   216→        \"n_bins_total\": int(keep.size),\n   217→        \"n_bins_kept\": int(keep.sum()),\n   218→        \"n_bins_moving\": int(moving.sum()),\n   219→        \"n_bins_unreachable\": int((moving & ~reachable).sum()),\n   220→        \"trial_index\": trial_index,\n   221→        \"env\": env_name,\n   222→        \"geometry\": geometry,\n   223→        \"bin_down\": bin_down,\n   224→    }\n   225→\n   226→    if show_processing:\n   227→        _plot_processing(session_id, raw, active, neural_binned, position_day, pos_binned,\n   228→                         xy_bin, partition, speed, speed_binned, keep, geometry, env_name,\n   229→                         bin_down, n_trials, plot_dir)\n   230→    return result\n   231→\n   232→\n   233→# python3\n\"\"\"\nConvert the Lee, Keinath, Cianfarano & Brandon (2025) CA1 geometric-deformation dataset\ninto the decoder-compatible pickle format.\n\n    Lee JQ, Keinath AT, Cianfarano E, Brandon MP (2025).\n    Identifying representational structure in CA1 to benchmark theoretical models of\n    cognitive mapping. Neuron 113(2):307-320.   Data: 10.5281/zenodo.13993254\n\nDecoder task\n------------\n    input  : environment geometry -- 9-dim binary vector, 1 = that 3x3 partition is\n             blocked off on this session.  Static within a trial.\n    output : the mouse's position, discretized into the 3 x 3 = 9 partitions of the\n             arena.  Time-varying, one categorical value (0..8) per time bin.\n    neural : CA1 calcium event rate (the paper's binarized rising-phase \"trace\",\n             treated as the firing rate), temporally binned.\n\nProcessing follows the reference implementation of the paper's own within-session\nposition decoding, `decode_position_within` / `fit_decoder` in\ncode/georepca1/src/utils.py:\n  * spatial binning        `bin_down = (max position over all days and both axes + buffer) / n_bins`\n  * running-speed filter   speed = gaussian_filter1d(|dposition|*fps, sigma=5 frames) > 5 cm/s\n  * cell filter            > 5 events among the retained (running) samples\n  * temporal binning       gaussian_filter1d(trace, sigma=bin) then average pooling by `bin`\nSee CONVERSION_NOTES.md for the full list of decisions and where/why this deviates.\n\nUsage\n-----\n    python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile> [--full | --sample] [--show-processing]\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport os\nimport pickle\nimport sys\nimport time\nfrom collections import OrderedDict\n\nimport joblib\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter1d\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Constants (values taken from the paper / reference code unless noted)\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nDATA_DIR = \"/app/data\"\n\nANIMALS = [\"QLAK-CA1-08\", \"QLAK-CA1-30\", \"QLAK-CA1-50\", \"QLAK-CA1-51\",\n           \"QLAK-CA1-56\", \"QLAK-CA1-74\", \"QLAK-CA1-75\"]          # main.py\n\nFPS = 30.0                 # methods: \"acquired behavioral and cellular imaging streams at 30 Hz\"\nTRIAL_SECONDS = 60.0       # decoder task: 1-minute trials\nTRIAL_FRAMES = int(round(TRIAL_SECONDS * FPS))          # 1800\nTEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES = 15   # 500 ms; reference uses 3 (see CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5 decision 4)\nBINS_PER_TRIAL = TRIAL_FRAMES // TEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES    # 120\nTIME_BIN_MS = TEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES / FPS * 1000.0        # 500.0\n\nV_FILT_SIGMA = 5.0         # decode_position_within(v_filt_size=5) frames\nV_THRESH = 5.0             # decode_position_within(v_thresh=5) cm/s\nCELL_EVENT_THRESHOLD = 5   # decode_position_within(cell_threshold=5) events\nSPATIAL_BINS = 3           # decoder task: 3 x 3 partitions (reference uses 15 for 5 cm bins)\nPOS_BUFFER = 1e-5          # get_rate_maps(buffer=1e-5)\n\nMIN_BINS_PER_TRIAL = 10    # 5 s of running; trials with less are dropped\nMIN_TRIALS_PER_SESSION = 2 # train_validate_decoder needs >= 2 trials to split a session\n\n# Two sessions used by --sample, chosen from two different animals and two geometries\n# so that the sample exercises subject indexing and a non-square geometry.\nSAMPLE_SESSIONS = [(\"QLAK-CA1-08\", 0), (\"QLAK-CA1-30\", 3)]\n\nBRAIN_REGIONS = [\"CA1\"]    # paper: GRIN lens targeting dorsal CA1\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Small helpers\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef parse_blocked(blocked_day):\n    \"\"\"Return the 9-dim binary geometry vector for one session.\n\n    The dataset's `blocked` field lists the indices of blocked (occluded) partitions in\n    the 3x3 grid, numbered [[0,1,2],[3,4,5],[6,7,8]] (dataset README), or -1 when nothing\n    is blocked (the square).  Verified in Step 4 to equal `flipud(get_env_mat(env)) == 0`\n    for every session, and to coincide exactly with the partitions the animal never\n    visits.\n\n    Returns:\n        geometry: (9,) float32, 1.0 where the partition is blocked.\n    \"\"\"\n    idx = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(blocked_day, dtype=float).ravel())\n    geometry = np.zeros(SPATIAL_BINS * SPATIAL_BINS, dtype=np.float32)\n    if idx.size == 1 and idx[0] < 0:          # -1 => open square, nothing blocked\n        return geometry\n    geometry[idx.astype(int)] = 1.0\n    return geometry\n\n\ndef compute_speed(position_xy):\n    \"\"\"Running speed in cm/s, exactly as in `decode_position_within`.\n\n    Args:\n        position_xy: (2, T) position in cm.\n    Returns:\n        (T,) speed; element 0 is 0 and is always excluded downstream, matching the\n        reference where `vel_idx[0]` is left False.\n    \"\"\"\n    step = np.linalg.norm(np.diff(position_xy, axis=1), axis=0) * FPS   # (T-1,) cm/s\n    speed = np.zeros(position_xy.shape[1], dtype=np.float64)\n    speed[1:] = gaussian_filter1d(step, sigma=V_FILT_SIGMA)\n    return speed\n\n\ndef bin_time(x, n_trials, axis=-1, reduce=\"mean\"):\n    \"\"\"Average-pool the last axis into (n_trials, BINS_PER_TRIAL) blocks of\n    TEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES frames.  Equivalent to torch AvgPool1d(kernel=stride=bin) applied\n    within each 1-minute trial.\"\"\"\n    x = np.moveaxis(x, axis, -1)\n    x = x[..., : n_trials * TRIAL_FRAMES]\n    shp = x.shape[:-1] + (n_trials, BINS_PER_TRIAL, TEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES)\n    out = x.reshape(shp).mean(axis=-1)\n    return out\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Per-session conversion\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef convert_session(trace_day, position_day, blocked_day, env_name, bin_down,\n                    session_id, show_processing=False, plot_dir=\".\"):\n    \"\"\"Convert a single animal-day recording into lists of per-trial arrays.\n\n    Args:\n        trace_day: (n_cells_total, T) binarized calcium events, NaN for cells that were\n                   not registered on this day.\n        position_day: (2, T) x-y position in cm.\n        blocked_day: the session's entry of the dataset `blocked` field.\n        env_name: geometry name (for bookkeeping / plots).\n        bin_down: spatial bin width in cm (shared across all sessions of the animal).\n        session_id: string used for plot filenames.\n    Returns:\n        dict with 'neural', 'input', 'output' (lists of per-trial arrays), plus\n        bookkeeping fields, or None if the session does not survive curation.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_cells_total, n_frames = trace_day.shape\n    n_trials = n_frames // TRIAL_FRAMES          # complete 1-minute trials only\n    if n_trials < MIN_TRIALS_PER_SESSION:\n        return None\n    n_used = n_trials * TRIAL_FRAMES\n\n    # ---- 1. cells registered on this day ------------------------------------------\n    registered = ~np.isnan(trace_day[:, 0])\n    raw = trace_day[registered][:, :n_used].astype(np.float32)   # (n_reg, n_used), {0,1}\n\n    # ---- 2. running speed and its temporal binning ---------------------------------\n    speed = compute_speed(position_day)\n    speed_binned = bin_time(speed[None, :], n_trials)[0]         # (n_trials, BINS_PER_TRIAL)\n\n    # ---- 3. position -> 3x3 partition index ----------------------------------------\n    pos_binned = bin_time(position_day, n_trials)                # (2, n_trials, BINS)\n    xy_bin = np.clip((pos_binned / bin_down).astype(int), 0, SPATIAL_BINS - 1)\n    partition = xy_bin[1] * SPATIAL_BINS + xy_bin[0]             # class = y_bin*3 + x_bin\n\n    # ---- 4. geometry (decoder input) ------------------------------------------------\n    geometry = parse_blocked(blocked_day)                        # (9,), 1 = blocked\n\n    # ---- 5. sample mask: running, and not in a physically blocked partition ---------\n    #   The second term removes rare tracking artifacts: a handful of frames (and, on\n    #   two sessions, a few hundred) are tracked inside a partition that is walled off,\n    #   which cannot happen physically.  These would be unlearnable label noise.\n    moving = speed_binned > V_THRESH\n    reachable = geometry[partition] == 0\n    keep = moving & reachable\n    keep[0, 0] = False          # frame/bin 0 has no defined speed (reference excludes it)\n\n    # ---- 6. cell activity filter ------------------------------------------------------\n    #   decode_position_within: cells with more than `cell_threshold` events among the\n    #   retained samples.  Counted on the raw binary events of the retained frames.\n    frame_keep = np.repeat(keep.ravel(), TEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES)\n    n_events = raw[:, frame_keep].sum(axis=1)\n    active = n_events > CELL_EVENT_THRESHOLD\n    if active.sum() == 0:\n        return None\n\n    # ---- 7. temporal binning of the neural data --------------------------------------\n    #   Reference recipe: gaussian smooth along time with sigma = bin size, then average\n    #   pool.  Expressed in Hz (x FPS) as in get_rate_maps.\n    smoothed = gaussian_filter1d(raw[active], sigma=TEMPORAL_BIN_FRAMES, axis=-1)\n    neural_binned = bin_time(smoothed, n_trials) * FPS           # (n_act, n_trials, BINS)\n    del smoothed\n\n    # ---- 8. assemble per-trial arrays -------------------------------------------------\n    neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials, trial_index = [], [], [], []\n    for t in range(n_trials):\n        sel = keep[t]\n        if sel.sum() < MIN_BINS_PER_TRIAL:\n            continue\n        neural_trials.append(np.ascontiguousarray(neural_binned[:, t, sel], dtype=np.float32))\n        input_trials.append(geometry.copy())\n        output_trials.append(partition[t, sel][None, :].astype(np.int64))\n        trial_index.append(t)\n\n    if len(neural_trials) < MIN_TRIALS_PER_SESSION:\n        return None\n\n    result = {\n        \"neural\": neural_trials,\n --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Per-animal driver\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef convert_animal(animal, days=None, show_processing=False, plot_dir=\".\"):\n    \"\"\"Load one animal's joblib file and convert the requested days.\"\"\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n    dat = joblib.load(os.path.join(DATA_DIR, animal))[animal]\n    t_load = time.time() - t0\n\n    trace = dat[\"trace\"] res[\"n_days\"] = int(n_days)\n        out.append(res)\n    t_proc = time.time() - t1\n\n    total_cells = trace.shape[1]\n    del dat, trace, position\n    print(f\"  {animal}: {len(out)} sessions, It: (1) computes `bin_down = (global max position over time, both axes and all days + buffer) / n_bins` → one isotropic spatial bin size shared by all sessions of an animal; (2) computes speed as `gaussian_filter1d(norm(diff(position)*fps), sigma=v_filt_size=5 frames)` and keeps only frames with **speed > 5 cm/s** (frame 0 always excluded); (3) keeps only cells with **> 5 events during the retained (moving) frames** (`cell_threshold=5`); (4) 5-fold cross-validation with `fit_decoder`/`test_decoder`. |\n    44→| `get_all_decoding_within` | `src/utils.py:1969` | ANALYSIS | Aggregates the saved `within_decoding` results (used for Figure 1F). |\n    45→\n    46→### Notes\n    47→- **No dF/F computation is needed.** `trace` in the distributed dataset is already the final\n    48→  *rise-extracted binarized* signal (values are exactly {0, 1}, verified). No further alignment is required.\n   125→- **Temporal binning** (reference decoder): traces smoothed with `gaussian_filter1d(sigma = temporal_bin_size)`\n   126→  along time, then non-overlapping average pooling with kernel = stride = `temporal_bin_size` frames;\n   127→  position average-pooled identically and then discretized.\n   128→- **Spatial binning**: `bin_down = (max position across both axes and all days + buffer) / n_bins`, i.e. an\n   129→  isotropic bin size shared across all sessions of an animal (= 25 cm for n_bins = 3).\n   130→\n   131→### Curation Steps\n   132→**Neuron curation rules** (as used by the paper's decoding analysis, `decode_position_within`):\n   133→1. Drop cells not registered on that day (all-NaN traces).\n   134→2. Drop cells with ≤ 5 events during the retained (running) frames of that session (`cell_threshold=5`).\n   135→3. **No place-cell selection** — the paper explicitly includes all cells (\"motivated the inclusion of all\n   136→   cells in subsequent analyses\").\n   137→\n   138→**Trial curation rules**: the original experiment has no trials (40 min of continuous free foraging).\n   139→The decoder task defines trials as 1-minute segments. Reference-derived sample curation:\n   140→- Only frames with running speed > 5 cm/s are used for position decoding (`v_thresh=5`,\n   141→  speed smoothed with `gaussian_filter1d(sigma=5 frames)`).\n   142→\n   143→### Decoders Trained\n   144→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   145→|---|---|\n   146→| Position (15×15 = 5 cm bins), Gaussian naive Bayes, 5-fold CV | mean Euclidean error 13.49 cm (chance for uniform occupancy in a 75 cm box ≈ 39 cm) |\n   147→No classification accuracy for a 3×3 discretization is reported in the paper, so there is no direct\n   148→accuracy target; the 13.5 cm mean error implies most predictions fall within the correct 25 cm partition\n   149→or an adjacent one.\n   150→\n   151→---\n   152→\n   153→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   154→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   155→\n   156→### Discrepancies Found\n   157→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   158→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   159→| Orientation of the 3×3 geometry matrix | `get_env_mat(env)` returns a 3×3 matrix with 0 = blocked | `blocked` indices equal `flipud(get_env_mat(env)) == 0`, flattened row-major (verified for every session of every animal) | README: partitions numbered `[[0,1,2],[3,4,5],[6,7,8]]` | `get_env_mat` is in *plot* orientation (row 0 = bottom, as used for the polygon outlines with `flipud=True` in `plots.py`); `blocked` is in data/array orientation. **I use `blocked` directly**, and verify it against actual occupancy (see Step 10 sanity checks). |\n   160→| Mapping from position to partition index | `fit_decoder` builds class = `x_bin * n_bins + y_bin` (row = x) ; `plots.py` displays `maps[...].T` | Occupancy is exactly zero in partition `p` iff `p ∈ blocked` when partition index = `y_bin * 3 + x_bin` | README partition layout | Use **class = y_bin * 3 + x_bin**, which is the indexing in which `blocked` (and therefore the paper's partition numbering) is expressed. This is the transpose of `fit_decoder`'s internal class index — an arbitrary relabelling of the 9 classes that leaves decoding accuracy unchanged, but makes the decoder *input* (geometry) and *output* (partition) share one index. |\n   161→| Spatial resolution | reference uses `n_bins=15` | — | 3×3 partition design of the apparatus | Task specifies 3×3 = 9 bins → `n_bins=3`, which coincides with the physical partition grid (25 cm bins). |\n   162→| Temporal bin | reference uses 3 frames (100 ms) | events are sparse (≈0.24 Hz/cell) | — | Increased to 15 frames (500 ms) — see Step 5 \"Key decisions\" for the empirical justification. |\n   163→| Number of days per animal | `main.py` implies up to 31 | 31 for 6 animals, 21 for QLAK-CA1-51 | \"up to three total repetitions (31 days)\" | Consistent — animal 51 completed only 2 sequences (21 days). |\n   164→| `.mat` vs joblib | `load_dat` supports both | both present in `data/` | — | Use the joblib files: `mat2joblib` shows they are a lossless conversion of the `.mat` files, and they load far faster. Verified by spot-checking values against the `.mat` file (Step 10). |\n   165→\n   166→Everything else is consistent: 7 animals × (31,31,31,21,31,31,31) days = 207 sessions; 5,413 cells;\n   167→69,744 registered cell-sessions; all numbers reproduce the paper exactly.\n   168→\n   169→---\n   170→\n   171→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   172→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   173→\n   174→### Variable Mapping\n   175→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   176→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   177→| `trace[day, cell, t]` (binary events) | `neural[session][trial]` (n_neurons, T) | drop unregistered (NaN) cells → drop cells with ≤5 events in retained frames → `gaussian_filter1d(sigma=15 frames)` along time → average-pool 15 frames (500 ms) → split into 1-min trials → keep bins with speed > 5 cm/s | `decode_position_within`, `fit_decoder` | float32 |\n   178→| `blocked[day]` | `input[session][trial]` (9,) | 9-dim binary vector, 1 = partition blocked; constant within a session | (`get_env_mat` equivalent) | static per trial, 1-D array |\n   179→| `position[day, :, t]` | `output[session][trial]` (1, T) | average-pool 15 frames → `floor(pos / 25 cm)`, clipped to [0,2] per axis → class = `y_bin*3 + x_bin` | `fit_decoder`, `decode_position_within` | int, 9 categories |\n   180→| animal ID | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | 7 IDs | `main.py` animal list | |\n   181→| — | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | all cells are dorsal CA1 | paper | `['CA1']`, all zeros |\n   182→| `envs[day]`, day index | `metadata['session_info']` | geometry name, day index, sequence number | | |\n   183→\n   184→### Key Decisions\n   185→1. **Session = one animal-day (40-min recording).** 207 sessions, matching the paper's unit of analysis.\n   186→   Different days have different registered cells, so neuron count varies per session (allowed by the format).\n   187→2. **Trials = consecutive, non-overlapping 60 s segments** (1800 frames at 30 Hz), as specified by the task.\n   188→   The trailing partial segment (< 60 s: 219, 91, 60, 71 frames for 5 animals; 1,666 frames ≈ 55 s for\n   189→   QLAK-CA1-30 and -50) is dropped so that every trial is a full minute. Maximum data loss 2.3 % for two\n   190→   animals, < 0.3 % for the rest. Result: 40 trials/session (39 for animals 30 and 50).\n   191→3. **Neural signal = the distributed binarized rising-phase `trace`.** No dF/F is needed: the pipeline in\n   192→   the paper already produced this binary event vector and \"treated [it] as the firing rate in all further\n   193→   analyses\".\n   194→4. **Temporal binning = 500 ms (15 frames), with the reference's smooth-then-average-pool recipe**\n   195→   (`gaussian_filter1d(sigma = bin) → AvgPool1d(kernel = stride = bin)`), so the neural value in a bin is a\n   196→   smoothed event *rate* (mean of the binary vector; multiplied by 30 to express it in Hz).\n   197→   *Why 500 ms rather than the reference's 100 ms*: calcium events are very sparse (≈0.24 Hz/cell), so a\n   198→   100 ms bin contains ~0.024 events/cell. The provided decoder is a linear read-out of 100 PCs evaluated\n   199→   per time bin, not a Bayesian accumulator, so per-bin SNR matters. Gaussian naive Bayes with flat priors. |\n| `test_decoder` | `src/utils.py:1806` | PROCESSING | Same binning on test data; error = Euclidean distance between predicted and true bin centres × bin size (cm). |\n| `decode_position_within(behav, traces, maps, n_bins=15, fps=30, v_filt_size=5, v_thresh=5, cell_threshold=5, buffer=1e-15, n_fold=5)` | `src/utils.py:1845` | CURATION + PROCESSING | **The paper's within-session position-decoding pipeline.** This is the single most relevant reference function. It: (1) computes `bin_down = (global max position over time, both axes and all days + buffer) / n_bins` → one isotropic spatial bin size shared by all sessions of an animal; (2) computes speed as `gaussian_filter1d(norm(diff(position)*fps), sigma=v_filt_size=5 frames)` and keeps only frames with **speed > 5 cm/s** (frame 0 always excluded); (3) keeps only cells with **> 5 events during the retained (moving) frames** (`cell_threshold=5`); (4) 5-fold cross-validation with `fit_decoder`/`test_decoder`. |\n| `get_all_decoding_within` | `src/utils.py:1969` | ANALYSIS | Aggregates the saved `within_decoding` results (used for Figure 1F). |\n\n### Notes\n- **No dF/F computation is needed.** `trace` in the distributed dataset is already the final\n  *rise-extracted binarized* signal (values are exactly {0, 1}, verified). The methods state this binary\n  vector \"was treated as the firing rate in all further analyses\". So the neural stream for the decoder is\n  this binary event train, temporally binned.\n- Cells that were not registered on a given day are stored as **all-NaN** along the time axis for that day\n  (verified: `np.isnan(trace).all(axis=time)` is identical to `np.isnan(trace[:, :, 0])`). These must be\n  dropped per session.\n- `blocked` is not used anywhere in the reference code (geometry is handled through the `envs` string names\n  and `get_env_mat`), but it encodes exactly the decoder input we need.\n- The reference decoding uses `n_bins=15` (5 cm bins). Our task specifies 3x3 = 9 bins, so `n_bins=3`\n  (25 cm partitions) — this matches the physical 3x3 partition design of the apparatus.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n`/app/data` contains, for each of 7 animals (`QLAK-CA1-08/30/50/51/56/74/75`):\n- `QLAK-CA1-XX` — joblib pickle, `{animal_id: {...}}` (used; loads in 9–23 s)\n- `QLAK-CA1-XX.mat` — the identical data as MATLAB v7.3 (source of the joblib file)\n\nPer-animal dictionary fields (n_days = sessions for that animal, T ≈ 72,000 frames @ 30 Hz = 40 min):\n\n| Field | Shape / type | Meaning |\n|---|---|---|\n| `trace` | (n_days, n_cells, T) float64 | Binarized rising-phase calcium events, {0,1}; **NaN for the whole day if the cell was not registered that day** |\n| `position` | (n_days, 2, T) float64 | x,y head position in **cm**, range exactly [0, 75]; no NaNs anywhere |\n| `envs` | (n_days, 1) `<U9` | Geometry name per day: square, o, t, u, rectangle, +, i, l, bit donut, glenn |\n| `blocked` | list of n_days | Indices of blocked partitions in the 3x3 grid laid out `[[0,1,2],[3,4,5],[6,7,8]]`; `-1` if none (square) |\n| `maps` | dict: `sampling` (15,15,n_days), `smoothed`/`unsmoothed` (15,15,n_cells,n_days) | Pre-computed occupancy and event-rate maps |\n| `SFPs` | (35,35,n_cells,n_days) | Spatial footprints |\n| `centroids` | (n_cells,2,n_days) | Footprint centroids |\n\n`data/precomputed_results/within_decoding` holds the paper's Figure-1F decoding errors\n(per animal: `decoding_error` (n_days, 5 folds)).\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Subjects (mice) | 7 |\n| Sessions (animal-days) | **207** (31,31,31,21,31,31,31) |\n| Sessions / subject | 21–31 (mean 29.6) |\n| Neurons (total, unique registered cells) | **5,413** (515, 875, 942, 554, 862, 713, 952) |\n| Neurons / session (registered, non-NaN) | mean **337.0** (= 69,744 / 207) |\n| Cell × session pairs (= rate maps) | **69,744** |\n| Session duration | 71,866 – 72,219 frames @ 30 Hz = 39.93 – 40.12 min |\n| Trials / session (1-min, complete) | 39 or 40 (see Step 5) |\n| Mean event rate per cell | 0.21–0.27 Hz (median ≈ 0.19 Hz) |\n| Fraction of frames with speed > 5 cm/s | 0.45–0.62 per animal (≈ 0.52 overall) |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons (total) | 5,413 | \"5,413 unique neurons across 207 sessions in 10 geometries, forming 69,744 rate maps\" |\n| Sessions | 207 | same |\n| Rate maps (cell × session) | 69,744 | same |\n| Geometries | 10 | same |\n| Subjects | 7 | animal list in `main.py`; No further alignment is required.\n- **Temporal binning** (reference decoder): traces smoothed with `gaussian_filter1d(sigma = temporal_bin_size)`\n  along time, then non-overlapping average pooling with kernel = stride = `temporal_bin_size` frames;\n  position average-pooled identically and then discretized.\n- **Spatial binning**: `bin_down = (max position across both axes and all days + buffer) / n_bins`, i.e. an\n  isotropic bin size shared across all sessions of an animal (= 25 cm for n_bins = 3).\n\n### Curation Steps\n**Neuron curation rules** (as used by the paper's decoding analysis, `decode_position_within`):\n1. Drop cells not registered on that day (all-NaN traces).\n2. Drop cells with ≤ 5 events during the retained (running) frames of that session (`cell_threshold=5`).\n3. **No place-cell selection** — the paper explicitly includes all cells (\"motivated the inclusion of all\n   cells in subsequent analyses\").\n\n**Trial curation rules**: the original experiment has no trials (40 min of continuous free foraging).\nThe decoder task defines trials as 1-minute segments. Reference-derived sample curation:\n- Only frames with running speed > 5 cm/s are used for position decoding (`v_thresh=5`,\n  speed smoothed with `gaussian_filter1d(sigma=5 frames)`).\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|---|---|\n| Position (15×15 = 5 cm bins), Gaussian naive Bayes, 5-fold CV | mean Euclidean error 13.49 cm (chance for uniform occupancy in a 75 cm box ≈ 39 cm) |\nNo classification accuracy for a 3×3 discretization is reported in the paper, so there is no direct\naccuracy target; the 13.5 cm mean error implies most predictions fall within the correct 25 cm partition\nor an adjacent one.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Orientation of the 3×3 geometry matrix | `get_env_mat(env)` returns a 3×3 matrix with 0 = blocked | `blocked` indices equal `flipud(get_env_mat(env)) == 0`, flattened row-major (verified for every session of every animal) | README: partitions numbered `[[0,1,2],[3,4,5],[6,7,8]]` | `get_env_mat` is in *plot* orientation (row 0 = bottom, as used for the polygon outlines with `flipud=True` in `plots.py`); `blocked` is in data/array orientation. **I use `blocked` directly**, and verify it against actual occupancy (see Step 10 sanity checks). |\n| Mapping from position to partition index | `fit_decoder` builds class = `x_bin * n_bins + y_bin` (row = x) ; `plots.py` displays `maps[...].T` | Occupancy is exactly zero in partition `p` iff `p ∈ blocked` when partition index = `y_bin * 3 + x_bin` | README partition layout | Use **class = y_bin * 3 + x_bin**, which is the indexing in which `blocked` (and therefore the paper's partition numbering) is expressed. This is the transpose of `fit_decoder`'s internal class index — an arbitrary relabelling of the 9 classes that leaves decoding accuracy unchanged, but makes the decoder *input* (geometry) and *output* (partition) share one index. |\n| Spatial resolution | reference uses `n_bins=15` | — | 3×3 partition design of the apparatus | Task specifies 3×3 = 9 bins → `n_bins=3`, which coincides with the physical partition grid (25 cm bins). |\n| Temporal bin | reference uses 3 frames (100 ms) | events are sparse (≈0.24 Hz/cell) | — | Increased to 15 frames (500 ms) — see Step 5 \"Key decisions\" for the empirical justification. |\n| Number of days per animal | `main.py` implies up to 31 | 31 for 6 animals, 21 for QLAK-CA1-51 | \"up to three total repetitions (31 days)\" | Consistent — animal 51 completed only 2 sequences (21 days). |\n| `.mat` vs joblib | `load_dat` supports both | both present in `data/` | — | Use the joblib files: `mat2joblib` shows they are a lossless conversion of the `.mat` files, and they load far faster. 1,666 frames ≈ 55 s for\n   QLAK-CA1-30 and -50) is dropped so that every trial is a full minute. The provided decoder is a linear read-out of 100 PCs evaluated\n   per time bin, not a Bayesian accumulator, so per-bin SNR matters.
